2. The sulfation of tyrosine residues is essential for their biological activity

Let me think about Zn-binding site geometry. Zn2+ typically forms tetrahedral coordination with 4 ligands. The ligands need to be spatially close in 3D structure even if they&#x27;re far apart in sequence.

In PDE-like domains:
- Zn2+ is typically coordinated by 1 His and 3 Asp/Glu residues, or 2 His and 2 Asp/Glu residues
- The specific pattern depends on the family

Looking at the structure:
- H33, H35, D37 are close together (within 4 residues) - these could form a metal-binding cluster
- D150 is about 115 residues away - could be in a loop that comes close in 3D
- H419/D420 are about 270 residues away from D150

For PDE-type Zn sites, the typical arrangement involves:
- 2 His from the metal-binding pocket
- 1 Asp from the metal-binding pocket  
- 1 additional residue from another part of the domain

Actually, in many PDE structures, Zn2+ coordination involves:
- His from the conserved H-x-H pattern
- Asp nearby
- His and/or Asp from other conserved positions

Looking at the sequence context:
- H33-S34-H35-M36-D37: This gives a potential H-H-D arrangement
- H114-D115-E116: Another potential H-D pair
- H418-H419-D420: Another potential H-D pair
